  Meta beat on revenue ($60.80B, +28% y/y, vs $60.29B) but missed badly on EPS ($6.18 vs $7.22, -13% y/y) as total costs rose 55% to $42.03B (including $2.40B legal and $1.18B severance), pushing operating margin to 31% from 43%; the critical number for the meta-capex question is FY26 capex narrowed to $130–145B from $125–145B — the floor moved up $5B — with $31.08B of capex plus finance leases in Q2 alone and total expenses now $165–169B. Free cash flow collapsed to $784M from $8.55B a year ago, which is precisely why the $14B El Paso data center is structured with BlackRock funds owning 80%: the mechanism to watch is that reported capex increasingly understates true compute additions as spend migrates into JVs/SPVs (inference). Ad engine is intact — impressions +14%, price per ad +12%, DAP 3.60B, Q3 guide $61–64B — so the raised capex floor is a demand-signal positive for NVDA, AVGO (MTIA custom silicon), VRT, and memory (000660/285A) even as it is an EPS negative for META, and it sets the bar for the rest of the Jul 28–31 hyperscaler window (hyperscaler-guides). Sentiment context: 84% of prediction traders expected a beat and shares still fell ~4%, another sell-the-beat datapoint relevant to the TSM question. Confirm with Q3 capex run-rate above the ~$32B/quarter implied pace and further 80%-owned JV announcements; refute if management signals the $145B top end is unreachable or GPU useful-life extension is used to flatter depreciation.

- `7` **AXT (NASDAQ:AXTI) Stock Jumps as AI-Wafer Demand Drives 65% Incremental Margin** → [[Theses/LITE - Lumentum]]
  AXT — not a coverage ticker but the InP substrate chokepoint — guided Q3 revenue to exactly $66M versus $38.8M pre-call consensus (+70%) and adjusted EPS to $0.30–0.32 vs $0.11, after Q2 revenue of $47.59M vs $34.08M expected, with 64.8% incremental gross margin, record quarterly indium-phosphide revenue and CEO Morris Young calling it an 'inflection point' driven by data-center optical demand. The hard commitment is the six-year InP capacity reservation with Lumentum: a $43.5M initial deposit (91% of AXT's entire Q2 revenue) plus a second $43.5M tranche with timing set during 2028, totaling $87M of credits against future shipments — a prepayment of that scale implies LITE sees multi-year EML/laser demand it cannot source spot, which is bullish for LITE volumes and for the cpo/inp-capacity theses (and supportive read-across to AAOI, IQE as alternate substrate/epi supply, and to the china-optics question). Critical second-order point for export-controls: China has restricted InP export licences since February 2025 and AXT makes most of its InP substrates there, with SemiAnalysis calling InP 'one of several supply chain bottlenecks collectively gating AI data centre buildouts' — so this is a capacity constraint that pricing alone cannot solve and a potential upside catalyst for non-China InP/epi capacity (IQE, Veeco-fed MOCVD builds). Quality caveats: net interest income was $4.7M, 31.2% of pretax profit, and diluted shares are up 45.2% YoY, so per-share leverage is diluted; confirmation would be permitted-shipment volumes converting the reservation into revenue, refutation a licence-timing slip.
  Sources: [ts2.tech](https://ts2.tech/en/axt-nasdaqaxti-stock-jumps-as-ai-wafer-demand-drives-65-incremental-margin/)
- `7` **AXT (NASDAQ:AXTI) surges 27% as record indium phosphide sales push gross margin higher** → [[Theses/LITE - Lumentum]]
  The detail behind the AXT print is an inflection-grade datapoint: revenue $47.6M (+165% y/y, +$20.7M q/q) with GAAP gross margin 44.9% vs 8.0% a year ago and ~64.8% incremental margin, and a Q3 guide of ~$66M vs $38.7M consensus (+70%) with EPS $0.30-0.32 vs $0.10 — i.e. guidance beats consensus by more than the quarter did, so the curve is steepening, not peaking. Management says backlog is >$100M stretching into 2027, demand exceeds capacity, and InP capacity goes from ~$60M/quarter exiting 2026 to ~$130M/quarter exiting 2027; Coherent prepaid $22.3M for three years of 6-inch substrates and Lumentum reserved six years with $43.5M upfront plus a 2028 payment. Two constraints matter for the read: CFO Fischer's ~$66M is achievable only under current export permits (additional volume needs approvals on an uncertain timeline), so this is also an export-controls story — permit flow, not fabs, sets the ceiling. Transmission: sustained InP tightness through 2027 underwrites LITE/Coherent laser pricing and makes IQE's InP franchise more valuable (iqe-bid, inp-capacity), and it is a leading indicator that the optics content step-up for CPO/1.6T is being pre-booked now; watch whether AXT's late-2027 $130M/quarter target implies enough supply to de-bottleneck, which would flip the pricing-power read.

- `7` **ARM (NASDAQ:ARM) Shares Up 8% After Better-Than-Expected Earnings**
  Arm's FQ1'27 print beat on both lines — revenue $1.29B (+22.4% y/y) vs ~$1.26B consensus and adj EPS $0.45 vs $0.40 — and the Q2 EPS guide of $0.43–0.51 sits well above the $0.39 consensus, driving an 8% move (traded as high as $268.48 vs the $224.89 prior close, last $242.85 on 48% above-average volume). The decision-useful detail is mix, not the beat: data-center royalty revenue reportedly doubled y/y while total Q2 royalty growth is guided to only ~13%, with management flagging smartphone weakness and memory-price pressure as the drag — i.e. AI/server royalty is carrying the model while the handset base decelerates. That doubling is a corroborating datapoint for the custom-CPU/XPU buildout thesis behind MRVL, AVGO and NVDA's Arm-based Grace/Vera hosts (touches openai-xpu and mrvl-fabric transmission: more non-Nvidia accelerator sockets means more Arm-licensed host CPUs), and the memory-cost callout independently supports the DRAM pricing-power read on 000660/285A — rising memory BOM is now visibly compressing downstream handset demand. Note the sell-side split: numbers up but multiples down (RBC cut to $340 from $475, Wells to $280 from $350, Morgan Stanley Equal Weight at $212 vs Citi Buy $300), which implies the debate is valuation/execution, not AI demand. Confirm with next quarter's explicit data-center royalty disclosure and whether royalty growth re-accelerates above the mid-teens; refute if the ~13% guide marks a plateau and smartphone/memory drag widens.

For PINS itself the commitment locks in cost but Madrigal's emphasis on 'hardware options and infrastructure efficiency' implies negotiated pricing flexibility; confirmation would be AWS quantifying Trainium capacity sold-out on its Q2 call (reporting the same day) or Pinterest disclosing rising cost-of-revenue guidance.

- `4` **AXT (AXTI) Is Down 11.3% After Major Indium Phosphide AI Deal And Q2 Earnings Beat** → [[Theses/LITE - Lumentum]]
  AXT's Q2 2026 print (revenue $47.59M, net income $11.13M) came with a long-term indium phosphide supply and capacity-reservation agreement with Lumentum Operations backed by up to $87.5M in customer deposits — a customer pre-funding supplier capacity, which is the classic signature of a hard physical bottleneck rather than ordinary contracting. Note the sources disagree sharply on the tape reaction: this item says AXTI is down 11.3% after the deal and beat, while the ts2 report has the stock +27% premarket on July 31 (implies either different windows or a fade after the initial spike — treat the price signal as unreliable, the contract as the fact). For coverage this is the inp-capacity question: InP substrate scarcity gates EML/DFB laser output for 800G/1.6T optics and, later, co-packaged optics, so it supports pricing power at LITE and epiwafer supplier IQE (and raises the strategic value behind the iqe-bid thesis), while adding a cost/supply risk for AAOI-type module assemblers. Confirmation would be LITE/Coherent naming substrate availability as a constraint on 1.6T ramps; refutation would be AXT capacity landing ahead of laser demand and margins normalising. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [finance.yahoo.com](https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/axt-axti-down-11-3-180742683.html)
- `3` **Live Nation Entertainment, Inc. (LYV) Q2 2026 Earnings Call Transcript**
  Headline-only: Live Nation's Q2 2026 earnings call transcript is available (see the substantive Q2 numbers in the adjacent item). *(headline only)*
  Sources: [news.google.com](https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMioAFBVV95cUxPOUtBM2MtaVdydkxkSUxHM0xubXFfbUxnQ29uNXVkMFcyME91a3VBZ3dod2FiY1VyQ3dmNS1iUDNOOF91RkRkbWotcTl3NnNieTctbm9kYkdGdEZIZGc5Nks3bGlYUnZnQXdaMG1wVjZVWU80b2h4cGtBd2lxalVZNHItQjJUY3hkTHZhbHRwRUtQcmdmcTgyQ25zZTlXYkZx?oc=5) · [seekingalpha.com](https://seekingalpha.com/article/4928259-live-nation-entertainment-inc-lyv-q2-2026-earnings-call-transcript)
- `2` **Marvell Technology Aktie: Amazon plant 220 Milliarden Dollar** → [[Theses/MRVL - Marvell Technology]]
  The stated trigger for Marvell's ~8% bounce (after roughly a third of value lost in 30 days) is Amazon raising 2026 capex 10% to $220B, with AWS revenue +37% to $42.2B and backlog cited at $496B; the piece frames MRVL's five-year AWS agreement (custom AI silicon, optical DSP, Ethernet switch silicon) as the conduit, against management's >$10B custom-silicon target by FY2029 and Q1 FY27 revenue of $2.418B (+28%) with data center at 76% of sales. Caveat: this is opinion-led German retail commentary (it also cites a €171.72 price, RSI, and a €222.92 consensus target), so treat the AWS capex and backlog figures as claimed rather than verified. For the mrvl-trainium question the mechanism that matters is whether a $220B AWS budget converts into Trainium unit volume where MRVL holds content versus Alchip/Annapurna in-house, and for hyperscaler-guides it is another datapoint that the Jul 28-31 window is a capex raise, not a digestion phase. Confirmation: MRVL's next print quantifying custom-compute ramp and Trainium socket detail; refutation: AWS emphasising Alchip/alternate ASIC partners or MRVL guiding custom flat while optics carries growth.
  Sources: [www.ad-hoc-news.de](https://www.ad-hoc-news.de/boerse/news/unternehmensnachrichten/marvell-technology-aktie-amazon-plant-220-milliarden-dollar/69906097)

## value

- `2` **Friday File: Amazon, AI CapEx, and the Clearest Value in Insurance**
  Confirms the sentiment side of the capex trade: Microsoft was rewarded this quarter for trimming full-year capex slightly and guiding to positive FCF for the year, while Meta — still raising capex with no direct monetization path — fell 5-10% post-print, the same pattern Alphabet showed a week earlier (since clawed back), and Apple's 'don't play' stance made it briefly the largest company before a ~10% drop on soft holiday guidance handed the crown back to NVDA. Read against item 2, this is the key bifurcation for the hyperscaler-guides question: absolute spending is accelerating (+$95-110B of added 2026 guidance since January) but the market is now paying for FCF discipline, which means AI supply-chain longs (NVDA, AVGO, MRVL, VRT, TSM, memory) face multiple compression risk on the very prints that raise their order books — the 'sell-the-beat' setup. For META specifically the sig is negative near-term (capex up, monetization unproven, stock already de-rated); the confirming datapoint to watch is whether any hyperscaler actually trims capex guidance rather than merely showing positive FCF — none did this quarter, so the author's own framing ('a blip of investor worry' vs 'peak of the spend-as-fast-as-you-can wave') remains unresolved. Note this is opinion/commentary, not new company disclosure.
  Sources: [Travis Johnson, Stock Gumshoe](https://www.stockgumshoe.com/2026/07/friday-file-4/)
- `2` **Is the AI Boom Changing the Semiconductor Cycle?**
  David Samra lays out the structural bear case on memory: at 85% gross margins the industry is 'clearly not bumping along the bottom of the cycle,' and with price above marginal cost both sides respond — customers engineer down usage while Samsung, Hynix and Micron add capacity in large, partly subsidized increments, plus Chinese self-sufficiency DRAM build (CXMT-type capacity) that, while not leading-edge, displaces sales others would have made. That is the direct counterweight to item 2's Amazon memory-cost datapoint: for 000660, 285A and SNDK the question is whether AI-driven scarcity (Amazon says capacity is short through 2027) outlasts the supply response, and Samra's honest answer is that supply-demand models are 'all over the map,' which he treats as itself a reason to doubt the risk/reward. Transmission: sustained 85% margins would keep semicap orders (AMAT, LRCX, KLA, ASMI, ONTO, CAMT, TER/Advantest for HBM test) rising; the refuting signal to watch is any 1c-DRAM/HBM4 capacity announcement that outruns hyperscaler bit demand, or contract price flattening — the confirming signal is more customers doing what Amazon just did and raising budgets rather than cutting memory content.
  Sources: [Johnny Hopkins](https://acquirersmultiple.com/2026/07/is-the-ai-boom-changing-the-semiconductor-cycle/)
